About 63% of Filipinos considers themselves poor by the fourth quarter of 2024 amidsts the rising inflation, which was the highest since 2003.[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211] Exports began to decline during his administration. Following an all-time high of nearly \$80 billion in 2022, it declined to \$74 billion in 2023, and \$68 billion in 2024.[^cite_note-212]



Marcos convenes a [NEDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Economic_and_Development_Authority) meeting at Malacañang Palace in December 2022, approving the Philippine Development Plan for 2023–2028.

The Marcos administration approved the inclusion of the Philippines to the [Regional Comprehensive Economic Partnership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Comprehensive_Economic_Partnership) (RCEP) [free trade agreement](2533562.md), which was ratified by the Senate on February 21, 2023.[^cite_note-213] His administration also formed the [Private Sector Advisory Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_Sector_Advisory_Council), a council that will advise the President on economic affairs.[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215]

Marcos issued an executive order suspending *e-sabong* (online cockfighting) operations in the country.[^cite_note-216] He also issued an executive order banning [Philippine offshore gaming operators](61693885.md) (POGOs), citing concerns for national security.[^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218] Residential condominiums and offices markets were severely affected following the POGO ban, which saw a vacancy rate of up to 20.5% following the surrender of spaces and non-renewals of leases from POGOs.[^cite_note-219]



Marcos with [European Commission](9974.md) president [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) and Swiss president [Alain Berset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alain_Berset) at the [World Economic Forum](172185.md) in [Davos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davos), Switzerland, in January 2023

In the early years of his presidency, he sought to prioritize the country's post-[COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_the_Philippines) economy as highlighted by his economic agenda,[^cite_note-Bongbong_Marcos_team_presents_8-point_economic_agenda-220] lifting the lockdowns and facemask restrictions. His administration sought to target a 6.5 to 7.5% [real gross domestic product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_gross_domestic_product) (GDP) growth rate, with a 6.5 to 8% annual real GDP growth rate, a 9% or single-digit [poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_in_the_Philippines) rate by 2028, a 3% national [government deficit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_deficit)-to-GDP ratio by 2028, lowering the country's [debt-to-GDP ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debt-to-GDP_ratio) to less than 60% by 2025, and securing an [upper middle-income status](78449.md) by 2024 with a [US\$](18717338.md)4,256 income per capita, which is part of his 2023–2028 fiscal strategy. Marcos also supports the creation of additional [economic zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_economic_zone) in various areas of the country to attract investments and laid out plans to impose [digital taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_tax) and improve the country's tax compliance procedures which should improve revenue collections and cut the country's debts, while maintaining the country's disbursements at above 20 percent of its GDP.[^cite_note-What_Marcos_said_in_his_first_SONA,_from_A-Z-221][^cite_note-Marcos_bares_plans_for_economic_recovery_in_first_SONA-222]

#### Insurgency

This section is an excerpt from [Presidency of Bongbong Marcos § Insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Bongbong_Marcos#Insurgency).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Presidency_of_Bongbong_Marcos\&action=edit)]

To harmonize the Bangsamoro peace agreements of the [1976 Tripoli Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Tripoli_Agreement), the [1996 Final Peace Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Final_Peace_Agreement) and the [2014 Comprehensive Agreement on the Bangsamoro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Agreement_on_the_Bangsamoro), the [Office of the Presidential Adviser on Peace, Reconciliation and Unity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_Presidential_Adviser_on_Peace,_Reconciliation_and_Unity) facilitated the participation of the [Moro National Liberation Front](2821886.md) (MNLF) in the [Bangsamoro Transition Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangsamoro_Transition_Authority) (BTA). On August 14, 2022, Marcos appointed new members of the BTA, and included Abdulkarim Misuari and Nurrheda Misuari, son and daughter of [Moro National Liberation Front](2821886.md) leader [Nur Misuari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nur_Misuari), in an effort to unite former warring members of the MNLF and the [Moro Islamic Liberation Front](51673.md) under one Bangsamoro autonomous government.[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224]

#### Communist policy

Amid the weakening of the communist fronts by the military, in May 2023, Marcos directed the "recalibration" of the [NTF-ELCAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NTF-ELCAC), an anti-insurgency task force, to shift its previous "aggressive" policy and become "bringers of peace".[^cite_note-225] Marcos appointed Vice President [Sara Duterte](42504861.md) as co-vice chair of the NTF-ELCAC.[^cite_note-226] As part of his administration's peace initiatives, in November 2023, Marcos granted amnesty to former rebels of the [Communist Party of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_the_Philippines)-[New People's Army](1262096.md)-[National Democratic Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democratic_Front_\(Philippines\)) (CPP-NPA-NDF), [Moro Islamic Liberation Front](51673.md) (MILF), and [Moro National Liberation Front](2821886.md) (MNLF); he also granted amnesty to members of the Rebolusyonaryong Partido ng Manggagawa ng Pilipinas/Revolutionary Proletarian Army/Alex Boncayao Brigade (RPMP-RPA-ABB).[^cite_note-227]

In a reversal of Duterte's policy, the Marcos administration and the NDF issued a joint statement on November 28, 2023, announcing the revival of peace talks between the two parties,[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-Presidency_of_Bongbong_Marcos_DiplomatStrangio-229] with the government side to be led by retired general [Emmanuel Bautista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmanuel_Bautista) and Special Presidential Assistant [Antonio Lagdameo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Lagdameo) and the NDF side to be led by [Luis Jalandoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Jalandoni).[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-Presidency_of_Bongbong_Marcos_DiplomatStrangio-229] Vice President Duterte asked Marcos to reconsider the plan to hold peace talks which she called an "agreement with the devil", while Speaker [Martin Romualdez](45505193.md) deemed the decision to be a "moral imperative" for the country.[^cite_note-231] Negotiations between the Marcos administration and the NDF began in [Oslo](22309.md) as early as 2022, but were withheld from the public.[^cite_note-232]

After the [Supreme Court](1335534.md) ruled in May 2024 that [red-tagging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-tagging_in_the_Philippines) threatens a person's right to life, liberty, or security, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) and [Karapatan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karapatan) called on Marcos to abolish the NTF-ELCAC. Marcos rejected the calls for abolition, saying the task force was instrumental in reducing the country's internal security threat.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234] In July 2024, Duterte resigned as co-vice chairperson of NTF-ELCAC.[^cite_note-235]

#### Telecommunications

This section is an excerpt from [Presidency of Bongbong Marcos § Telecommunications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Bongbong_Marcos#Telecommunications).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Presidency_of_Bongbong_Marcos\&action=edit)]

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(June 2024)* |
| --- | --- |

In 2022, the Marcos administration launched the BroadBand ng Masa Program (BBMP) to provide free WiFi connections in remote areas.[^cite_note-236] To "boost government initiatives against scams committed through text and online messages", Marcos signed [his first law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SIM_Registration_Act), which mandated [SIM card](197489.md) registration;[^cite_note-237] by the end of the July 2023 deadline, a total of 113.9 million SIM cards were registered, and about 54 million unregistered mobile numbers were deactivated.[^cite_note-238]

### Foreign policy

See also: [List of international presidential trips made by Bongbong Marcos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_presidential_trips_made_by_Bongbong_Marcos)



Marcos addresses the [77th session of the United Nations General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seventy-seventh_session_of_the_United_Nations_General_Assembly) on September 20, 2022.



Marcos speaks with Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) during [the latter's visit to Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_presidential_trips_made_by_Volodymyr_Zelenskyy) on June 3, 2024.



Marcos meets with Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) during a visit to [Kazan](202295.md) on June 18, 2026.

Early in his presidency, Marcos promised to continue his predecessor [Rodrigo Duterte](7407293.md)'s foreign policy of being "friends to all, enemies to none".[^cite_note-239] Marcos initially sought [closer ties with China](33734272.md),[^cite_note-240] but has since been increasingly seen as more pro-American than Duterte in an attempt to create a centrist-style balancing act between the two superpowers.[^cite_note-Marcos:_PH_rejects_claims_SCS_disputes_due_to_rivalry_of_2_'powerful_countries'-241][^cite_note-The_Philippines’_Quest_for_Balance:_Marcos’_Foreign_Policy-242][^cite_note-The_Philippines_Is_America’s_New_Star_Ally_in_Asia-243][^cite_note-Marcos_Jr_fashions_a_flexible_foreign_policy_for_the_Philippines-244][^cite_note-245] During his first [State of the Nation Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_State_of_the_Nation_Address_\(Philippines\)), Marcos promised to "not preside over any process that will abandon even one-square inch of territory of the Republic of the Philippines to any foreign power".[^cite_note-Bongbong_Marcos_stands_firm_on_protecting_PH_waters-246]

Under his presidency, Marcos intensified the Philippines' cooperation on both economic and defense arrangements to [Western countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_countries), such as the United States, Japan, Australia, and the [European Union](9317.md), while strengthening its defense posture within the region.[^cite_note-Sea_Change:_‘Bongbong’_Marcos_and_the_Future_of_the_South_China_Sea-247][^cite_note-Philippine_President_Marcos_Jr.’s_Foreign_Policy_Emphasizes_Cooperation-248] Marcos approved the designation of four additional bases to be used by the United States military under the [Enhanced Defense Cooperation Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enhanced_Defense_Cooperation_Agreement).[^cite_note-249] In May 2024, the Philippines and the United States held its largest [Balikatan](42668628.md) military exercises, fueling concerns from local civilians who fear they would be affected in any future war between the US and China.[^cite_note-250] The deployment of the United States' Typhon Weapons System in an undisclosed location in northern [Luzon](69375.md) also caught the attention of Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md), who said that Russia should resume producing nuclear-capable missiles and consider where to deploy them.[^cite_note-251]

Marcos called on all involved parties on the South China Sea to abide by the 1982 [United Nations Convention on the Law of the Sea](31940.md) in order to diffuse potential conflicts in the future.[^cite_note-Bongbong_Marcos:_Abide_by_Unclos_to_prevent_conflict_in_South_China_Sea-252] Due to Marcos's "transparency thrust" in dealing with the aggressive actions of the [Chinese Coast Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Coast_Guard) and the Chinese [Maritime Militia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_Militia), [China–Philippines relations](33734272.md) have significantly deteriorated during Marcos's tenure, with increasing tensions over [territorial disputes in the South China Sea](32005536.md)[^cite_note-Philippines_strikes_security_deals_as_tensions_rise_with_China_at_sea-253][^cite_note-254] and the Philippines withdrawing from the [Belt and Road initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belt_and_Road_initiative).[^cite_note-255]

### Human rights

This section is an excerpt from [Human rights in the Philippines § Marcos Jr. administration (2022–present)](4513322.md#Marcos_Jr._administration_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Human_rights_in_the_Philippines\&action=edit)]

President Bongbong Marcos promised to uphold human rights during his term.[^cite_note-Human_rights_in_the_Philippines_\:3-256] The Philippines stated in 2022 that it was committed to cooperate with United Nations human rights mechanisms. The United Nations noted the failure of law enforcement to address human rights violations and recommended that the Philippines review all killings in the government's drug war.[^cite_note-257]

Drug war killings, attacks on activists, harassment of media, and red-tagging have continued. During the first year of Bongbong Marcos's term, there were 336 drug war killings, mostly during police operations,[^cite_note-Human_rights_in_the_Philippines_\:3-256] and 8 cases of forced disappearances.[^cite_note-Human_rights_in_the_Philippines_\:4-258] In 2024, the Commission on Human Rights said that human rights violations under the Duterte and Bongbong Marcos presidencies were almost similar.[^cite_note-Human_rights_in_the_Philippines_\:10-259] The Defend NGO Alliance stated that the human rights situation may have worsened under Bongbong Marcos. The alliance called for the abolition of repressive agencies and policies, such as the National Task Force to End Local Communist Armed Conflict and the Anti-Terrorism Act.[^cite_note-260]

As of November 2024, four journalists have been killed while doing their jobs under the Bongbong Marcos presidency. This includes broadcaster [Percy Lapid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Percy_Lapid) who was red-tagged and subsequently killed near his home. Journalists also continue to be red-tagged, harassed, or jailed, such as [Frenchie Mae Cumpio](81152351.md), who is detained on allegations of terror financing.[^cite_note-Human_rights_in_the_Philippines_\:10-259]

Congress representatives and human rights groups have urged the Bongbong Marcos administration to rejoin the Rome Statute and the International Criminal Court.[^cite_note-261] As of March 2025, the government has refused to rejoin the International Criminal Court.[^cite_note-262]

### Impeachment complaint

On January 19, 2026, an impeachment complaint was filed against Marcos before the [House of Representatives](293605.md) on charges related to the arrest of Rodrigo Duterte, his alleged drug use and involvement in the flood control scandal and issues over the national budget. The complaint was filed by lawyer Andre de Jesus and endorsed by [Pusong Pinoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pusong_Pinoy) partylist representative [Jett Nisay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jett_Nisay?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-263]

## Court cases

### Income and estate tax case convictions



Protests against Marcos over his involvement in tax evasion in September 2022

On June 27, 1990, a special tax audit team of the [Bureau of Internal Revenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Internal_Revenue_\(Philippines\)) (BIR) investigated the tax liabilities and obligations of the late Ferdinand Marcos, who died on September 29, 1989. The investigation disclosed in a 1991 memorandum that the [Marcos family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcos_family) had failed to file [estate tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estate_tax) returns and several [income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_tax) returns covering the years of 1982 to 1986 in violation of the National Internal Revenue Code.[^cite_note-gr-120880-264]

The BIR also issued a deficiency estate tax assessment against the estate of the late Ferdinand Marcos in 1991 for unpaid estate taxes from 1982 to 1985, and 1985 to 1986, totaling ₱23,293,607,638 (₱97,792,696,739 in 2022). Formal assessment notices were served to Bongbong Marcos at his office at the [Batasang Pambansa Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batasang_Pambansa_Complex) on October 20, 1992, who was then the representative of the 2nd District of Ilocos Norte. Several notices of [levy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax) were also issued by the BIR February 22, 1993, to May 26, 1993, to satisfy the deficiency of estate tax returns, to no avail.[^cite_note-gr-120880-264]

On March 12, 1993, lawyer Loreto Ata, representing Bongbong Marcos, called the attention of the BIR to notify them of any action taken by the BIR against his client. Bongbong Marcos then filed an instant petition on June 25, 1993, for *[certiorari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Certiorari)* and prohibition to contest the estate tax deficiency assessment.[^cite_note-gr-120880-264]

On July 27, 1995, [Quezon City Regional Trial Court](36842278.md) Judge Benedicto Ulep convicted Marcos to seven years in jail and a fine of US\$2,812 (₱138,491 in 2025) plus back taxes for [tax evasion](2256795.md) in his failure to file an [income tax return](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_tax_return) from the period of 1982 to 1985 while sitting as the vice governor of Ilocos Norte (1980–1983) and as governor of Ilocos Norte (1983–1986).[^cite_note-265] Marcos subsequently appealed the decision to the [Court of Appeals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Appeals_of_the_Philippines) over his conviction. However, in 1994, the Court of Appeals ruled that the estate tax deficiency assessment had become "final and unappealable", allowing it to be enforced.[^cite_note-isko-marcos-estate-tax-266]

On October 31, 1997, the Court of Appeals affirmed its earlier decision with Marcos being convicted for the failure of the filing of an income tax return under Section 45 of the National Internal Revenue Code of 1977 while being acquitted of tax evasion under the charge of violating Section 50 of the same statute. In spite of the removal of the penalty of imprisonment, Marcos was ordered the payment of [back income taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Back_taxes) to the [Bureau of Internal Revenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Internal_Revenue_\(Philippines\)) (BIR) with interest and the issuance of corresponding fines of ₱2,000 per count of non-filing of income tax returns from 1982 to 1984 and ₱30,000 for 1985, plus the accrued interest.[^cite_note-267] Marcos later filed a petition for [certiorari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Certiorari) to the [Supreme Court of the Philippines](1335534.md) over the modified conviction imposed by the Court of Appeals but subsequently withdrew his petition on August 8, 2001, thereby declaring the ruling as final and executory.[^cite_note-268]

In 2021, the Quezon City Regional Trial Court certified that there were no records on file of Marcos settling the corresponding tax dues and fines.[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270] However, according to Marcos's campaign team, documents issued by the Supreme Court, the BIR, and a receipt issued by the [Land Bank of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_Bank_of_the_Philippines) state that the tax dues were paid,[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] while elections commissioner [Rowena Guanzon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rowena_Guanzon) noted that the documents Marcos submitted to the [Commission on Elections](1847922.md) were not receipts of taxes paid to the BIR but rather receipts from the Land Bank for lease rentals.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274] Nevertheless, the [Commission on Elections](1847922.md) ruled against the consolidated disqualification cases against Marcos and stated that "Further, to prove the absence of any ill-intention and bad faith on his part," Marcos submitted a Bureau of Internal Revenue certification and an official receipt from the Landbank, showing his compliance with the CA decision directing him to pay deficiency income taxes amounting to a little over ₱67,000, including fines and surcharges.[^cite_note-275]

The estate tax deficiency assessment issued by the BIR has remained uncollected since the Supreme Court ruling on October 12, 1991. Since the ruling of the Supreme Court in 1997 which had junked the petition of Marcos to contest the estate tax deficiency assessment, under the [Ramos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Fidel_Ramos), [Arroyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Gloria_Macapagal_Arroyo), [Aquino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Benigno_Aquino_III), and [Duterte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte) administrations, the BIR has issued renewed written demands on the Marcos family to pay the estate tax liabilities, which has remained unpaid. As a result, the estate tax deficiency assessment, with penalties, is estimated to have ballooned to ₱203,819,066,829 (₱203.819 billion) as of 2021.[^cite_note-marocs-estate-tax-debt-276]

The unpaid estate tax return was used as grounds in one petition to cancel Marcos's certificate of candidacy for president in the 2022 elections. On March 1, 2022, presidential candidate and Manila mayor [Isko Moreno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isko_Moreno) said that he would implement the Supreme Court ruling ordering the Marcos family to pay their estate tax debts if elected, vowing to use the proceeds as relief aid (*ayuda*) for victims of the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_the_Philippines).[^cite_note-isko-marcos-estate-tax-266] [Ernest Ramel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernest_Ramel), the secretary general of Aksyon Demokratiko, the party of Moreno continuously called out BIR about the issue.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-\:1-278]

On March 28, 2022, Senator [Koko Pimentel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koko_Pimentel) filed Senate Resolution No. 998, stating an urgent and pressing need for the Senate to look into why the estate tax has remained uncollected for almost 25 years, which the amount has already been ruled to be due and demandable against the heirs of his father.[^cite_note-pimentel-senate-resolution-279]

### 2007 Payanig sa Pasig property case motion

Further information: [Payanig sa Pasig land dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Payanig_sa_Pasig_land_dispute)

On June 19, 2007,[^cite_note-hrclaimpayanig-280] Marcos Jr. filed a motion to intervene in, OCLP v. PCGG, Civil Case Number 0093 at the Sandiganbayan, the Philippines' anti-graft court.[^cite_note-hrclaimpayanig-280] The case had been filed by Ortigas & Company, Ltd. Partnership (OCLP) against the Presidential Commission on Good Government (PCGG) over the 18-hectare (44-acre) former Payanig sa Pasig property bordering [Ortigas Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ortigas_Avenue), [Julia Vargas Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Vargas_Avenue), and [Meralco Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meralco_Avenue) in [Ortigas Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ortigas_Center), [Pasig](359702.md), which had been the site of the 'Payanig sa Pasig' theme park, but is now the location of various businesses, most notably the [Metrowalk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metrowalk) shopping and recreation complex.[^cite_note-abscbnpayanigblock-281]

The PCGG considers the property the "crown jewel" among the properties sequestered from the Marcoses' ill-gotten wealth, estimating its minimum value to be about ₱16.5 billion in March 2015.[^cite_note-PCGGSellPayanig2015-282] The property had been surrendered to the PCGG in 1986, as part of the settlement deal of Marcos crony [Jose Yao Campos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jose_Yao_Campos), who was holding the property under various companies on Marcos Sr.'s behalf.[^cite_note-CamposAssets-283] Ortigas & Company countered that Marcos Sr. had coerced them to sell the property to him in 1968.[^cite_note-abscbnpayanigblock-281] Marcos Jr.'s motion claimed that his father had bought the property legally, but the Sandiganbayan dismissed his motion on October 18, 2008, saying it had already dismissed a similar motion filed years earlier by his mother Imelda.[^cite_note-sonappealspayanig-284]

### 2011 Hawaii contempt judgement

In 2011, the Hawaii District Court ruled Bongbong Marcos and his mother Imelda Marcos to be in contempt,[^cite_note-PDIBongbongImeldaLoseAppeal-285] fining them US\$353.6 million (₱17,414,799,999.93 in 2025) fine for not respecting an injunction from a 1992 judgement in a human rights victims case, which commanded them not to dissipate the assets of Ferdinand Marcos's estate.[^cite_note-Honolulu_Civil_Beat-286][^cite_note-EnforceUSImeldaBongbongRuling-287] The ruling was upheld by the [US Ninth Circuit Court of Appeals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Ninth_Circuit) on October 24, 2012, and is believed to be "the largest contempt award ever affirmed by an appellate court."[^cite_note-EnforceUSImeldaBongbongRuling-287] While the 1992 case was against Ferdinand Marcos, the 2011 judgment was against Imelda and Bongbong personally.[^cite_note-PDIMarcosesInContempt-288] The judgement also effectively barred Imelda and Bongbong from entering any US territory.[^cite_note-PDIBongbongImeldaLoseAppeal-285] However, on June 9, 2022, [United States Deputy Secretary of State](401375.md) [Wendy Sherman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wendy_Sherman)[^cite_note-289] clarified in a roundtable discussion with local reporters during a state visit, that as a head of state, Marcos enjoys [diplomatic immunity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic_immunity) in all circumstances, stating that he is welcome to visit the United States under his official role.[^cite_note-290]

The Marcos couple meets Chinese leader [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) and [Peng Liyuan](10462776.md) in [Beijing](18603746.md) in January 2023.

Marcos has described his political views as "[conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative)" and "[Machiavellian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machiavelli)".[^cite_note-291] He has also described the "spirit of [nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalism)" as a force driving progress in the Philippines.[^cite_note-292] Before taking office, Marcos has been described in media reports as a [populist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Populist).[^cite_note-293][^cite_note-294] On social issues, he is in favor of legalizing abortion for rape and incest victims[^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296] as well as divorce and same-sex unions.[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298] Marcos is also opposed to reinstating the death penalty for convicted heinous criminals[^cite_note-299] and lowering the minimum age of criminal responsibility to 12.[^cite_note-300] In terms of economic policy, analysts initially speculated that Marcos would pursue liberal policies as opposed to his protectionist rhetoric during the presidential campaign.[^cite_note-301]

### Marcos–Duterte feud

See also: [UniTeam § Aftermath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UniTeam#Aftermath)



Marcos with his predecessor, [Rodrigo Duterte](7407293.md), at Malacañang in August 2023

Amid the feud of the Marcos and Duterte clans in late April 2023, House Speaker and Marcos's cousin [Martin Romualdez](45505193.md) said the House of Representatives will probe into an agreement former president [Rodrigo Duterte](7407293.md) made during [his presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte) with Chinese leader [Xi Jinping](2017814.md). Under the agreement, Duterte agreed to maintain the "status quo" in the South China Sea to avoid escalating a war. Political analyst Ronald Llamas said the probe was engineered by Marcos as a "political payback" to Duterte's verbal attacks and to reduce Duterte's political influence ahead of the 2025 midterm elections.[^cite_note-302] The [2025 Philippine general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Philippine_general_election) saw mixed to unfavorable results for Marcos.

In 2024, Duterte criticized the Marcos administration's curtailment of the [freedom of speech](21401843.md) in violation of the 1987 [Bill of Rights](19554336.md). Duterte's nationwide "*Hakbang ng Maisug*" (brave) [prayer rallies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prayer_protest), which Duterte said the Marcos administration deliberately aimed to thwart, suffered setbacks and cancellations.[^cite_note-HnM-303][^cite_note-304] Duterte, however, said he prayed for Marcos to survive until the end of his term since Duterte does not want himself or his daughter Sara to become president.[^cite_note-HnM-303]

In June 2024, Vice President Sara Duterte resigned from the Marcos cabinet and grew critical of Marcos.[^cite_note-\:0-305] Her first public criticism of Marcos was during the 39th anniversary celebration of [Kingdom of Jesus Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Jesus_Christ) within [Davao City](489401.md) in late August 2024 during the [police search for Apollo Quiboloy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_of_Apollo_Quiboloy), where she admitted her regret in supporting Marcos' candidacy as president.[^cite_note-306] In November 2024, Philippine authorities [subpoenaed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subpoena) Vice President Duterte after she threatened to have Marcos, his wife Liza, and House Speaker Romualdez assassinated if she were killed. Without naming Duterte, Marcos condemned any sort of "criminal plans" and increased his security, while National Security Adviser [Eduardo Año](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduardo_A%C3%B1o) called the threats a national security concern.[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308] She was ordered to appear before the [National Bureau of Investigation](2489044.md), with such statements potentially leading to criminal charges.[^cite_note-\:0-305] Marcos, however, expressed openness to the possibility of reconciling with Duterte.[^cite_note-309] Despite growing calls for Sara Duterte's impeachment,[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] Marcos has repeatedly voiced his opinion against impeaching her, calling it "a storm in a teacup" and considering the move inconsequential to the lives of Filipinos.[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316]

On March 11, 2025, [Rodrigo Duterte was detained](79430656.md) in Manila, following an [arrest warrant](68742643.md) from the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) stemming from an investigation of human rights abuses during Duterte's anti-drug campaign.[^cite_note-317]

## Public profile

See also: [Opinion polling on the Bongbong Marcos presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_on_the_Bongbong_Marcos_presidency)

In 2024, *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* magazine listed him as one of [the world's 100 most influential people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100).[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319]

### Historical distortionism

Main article: [Historical distortion regarding Ferdinand Marcos § Historical denialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_distortion_regarding_Ferdinand_Marcos#Historical_denialism)

As with other Marcos family members who have stayed in the public eye since their return to the Philippines,[^cite_note-CMFR20160310-320][^cite_note-MerlinaHernandoMalipot20180907-321][^cite_note-NikDeYnchausti20160923-322] Marcos has received significant criticism for instances of [historical denialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_denialism), and his trivialization of the human rights violations and economic plunder that took place during the Marcos administration, and of the role he played in the administration.[^cite_note-amnestyInternational1975-323][^cite_note-singaporeSwissFunds-324][^cite_note-rapplerNoApologies-325] Specific criticisms have been leveled at Marcos for being unapologetic for human rights violations[^cite_note-amnestyInternational1975-323] and ill-gotten wealth[^cite_note-singaporeSwissFunds-324] during [his father](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_Marcos)'s administration.[^cite_note-unfazed-326][^cite_note-noDownsides-327][^cite_note-gmaBongToCritics-328][^cite_note-rapplerNoApologies-325] Of the human rights victims, Marcos Jr. said of them in 1999: "They don't want an apology, they want money."[^cite_note-onlyWantMoney-329] He then proceeded to state that his family would apologize only if they had done something wrong.

When victims of [human rights abuses during his father's administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_Marcos#Human_rights_abuses) commemorated the 40th year of the proclamation of martial law in 2012, Marcos Jr. dismissed their calls for an apology for the atrocities as "self-serving statements by politicians, self-aggrandizement narratives, pompous declarations, and political posturing and propagand&#x61;*."*[^cite_note-mlLateStrongmanSon-330][^cite_note-enrilesMemoir-331] In the *[Sydney Morning Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Morning_Herald)* later that year, Bongbong cited the various court decisions against the Marcos family as a reason not to apologize for Martial Law abuses, saying "we have a judgment against us in the billions. What more would people want?"[^cite_note-SydneyMorningHerald20121124-24]

During his 2016 vice presidential campaign, Marcos responded to then-president [Benigno Aquino III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benigno_Aquino_III)'s criticism of the Marcos regime and call to oppose his election run. He dismissed the events, saying Filipinos should "leave history to the professors."[^cite_note-inqLetHistorians-332][^cite_note-gmaLeaveItToProfs-333][^cite_note-absLetHistorians-334] This prompted over 500 faculty, staff and history professors from the [Ateneo de Manila University](330339.md) to immediately issue a statement condemning his dismissive retort as part of "an ongoing willful distortion of our history," and a "shameless refusal to acknowledge the crimes of the Martial Law regime."[^cite_note-AdMUStatement-335][^cite_note-AdMUStatementPDF-336][^cite_note-AteneoPresidents-337][^cite_note-inqAteneoProfsSlam-338][^cite_note-interaksyonAteneoProfsThumbDown-339] More than 1,400 Catholic schools, through the Catholic Educational Association of the Philippines (CEAP), later joined the call of the Ateneo faculty "against the attempt of \[Marcos] to canonize the harrowing horrors of martial rule."[^cite_note-ceapStatement-340][^cite_note-abs1400-341] This was also followed by the [University of the Philippines Diliman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_the_Philippines_Diliman)'s Department of History, which released a statement of its own, decrying what they called a "dangerous" effort for Marcos to create "myth and deception."[^cite_note-fbUPHistoryDeptStatement-342][^cite_note-inqMarcosDeception-343][^cite_note-PhilStarUpProfsSlam-344]

On September 20, 2018, Marcos Jr. released a YouTube video showing a [tête-à-tête](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/t%C3%AAte-%C3%A0-t%C3%AAte) between him and former senate president [Juan Ponce Enrile](3322486.md), who had been his father's defense minister before playing a key role in his ouster during the 1986 EDSA revolution.[^cite_note-PatriciaLourdesViray20180921-345] The video made a number of claims, which were quickly refuted and denounced by martial law victims, including former senate president [Aquilino Pimentel Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquilino_Pimentel_Jr.), former [DSWD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Social_Welfare_and_Development) secretary [Judy Taguiwalo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judy_Taguiwalo), former Commission on Human Rights chair [Etta Rosales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etta_Rosales), and writer [Boni Ilagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boni_Ilagan), among others. Enrile later backpedaled from some of his claims, attributing them to "unlucid intervals."[^cite_note-PaoloRomero20181026-346]

### Online presence

According to research by [Vera Files](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vera_Files), Marcos benefited the most from fake news from the Philippines in 2017, along with President Rodrigo Duterte.[^cite_note-VeraFiles-2017-347] Most viral news were driven by shares on networks of Facebook pages.[^cite_note-VeraFiles-2017-347] Also, most Philippine audience Facebook pages and groups spreading online disinformation bore "Duterte", "Marcos" or "News" in their names and are pro-Duterte.[^cite_note-348]

In July 2020, [Brittany Kaiser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brittany_Kaiser) alleged in an interview that Marcos had approached the controversial firm [Cambridge Analytica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambridge_Analytica) in order to "rebrand" the Marcos family image on social media.[^cite_note-349] Marcos's spokesperson Vic Rodriguez denied these allegations and stated that Marcos is considering filing libel charges against [Rappler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rappler), which published Kaiser's interview.[^cite_note-350]

### Impostor urban legend

Between the late 70s and early 80s, an urban legend became popular claiming that Marcos Jr. was stabbed and died during a scuffle while studying abroad. The Marcos family allegedly looked for Bongbong's look-alike to replace him. This was later debunked by Marcos in one of his vlogs. The origins of this urban legend remain unknown.[^cite_note-351]

### Tallano gold myth

See also: [Tallano gold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallano_gold)

In 1990, during a coverage of Imelda Marcos's trial in New York, [Inquirer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Daily_Inquirer) journalist Kristina Luz interviewed then-33-year-old exiled Bongbong Marcos and asked where the Marcos wealth came from. Marcos responded "only I know where the gold is and how to get it". This was corroborated in a 1992 report by the [Associated Press](18935732.md) that quoted Imelda Marcos saying that her husband's wealth came "from the Japanese and other gold he found after [World War II](32927.md), and not from the Philippine coffers." In 2007, Marcos informed the anti-graft court [Sandiganbayan](8563865.md) that his [father](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_Marcos)'s wealth came from trading "precious metals more specifically gold from the years 1946 to 1954" when he tried to win back the [Ortigas Payanig property in Pasig](#2007_Payanig_sa_Pasig_property_case_motion) from the national government.[^cite_note-352]

The myth surrounding the gold allegedly owned by the Marcos family has been the subject of various misinformation, as in 2011, a Facebook post claimed that a certain "Tallano clan" had paid Ferdinand Marcos in gold for his legal services. Several years later, supporters of the Marcos family in a Facebook page called "Marcos Cyber Warriors" also claimed that Marcos Sr.'s wealth came from his former law client, the "Maharlikan Tallano family".[^cite_note-InqTallano-353]

This has resulted in a long-running belief that should Bongbong Marcos win as president, he will give Filipinos a share of this gold. However during his [Philippine presidential election campaign in the 2022 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Philippine_presidential_election), when asked over [One News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_News_\(TV_channel\)) to verify the mythical "[Tallano gold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallano_gold)" or the long-believed tale that they got a share of the Japanese [Yamashita gold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yamashita_gold), Marcos denied knowledge of it, even joking that "people should let him know if they see any of that gold". The urban myth had allegedly been suggested or carried by various social media pages being run by Marcos supporters in order to engage more people to support his presidential bid.[^cite_note-InqTallano-353]

## Personal life



The [first family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_family_of_the_Philippines) at the sideline of the [2026 State of the Nation Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_State_of_the_Nation_Address_\(Philippines\)) on July 27



Marcos (center) inspecting the [San Juanico Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Juanico_Bridge) with his eldest son, [Sandro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandro_Marcos) (right), and his cousin, [Tacloban](746499.md) mayor [Alfred Romualdez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Romualdez) (left), on June 11, 2025.

Marcos is married to lawyer [Louise "Liza" Cacho Araneta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louise_Araneta%E2%80%93Marcos), a member of the prominent [Araneta family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Araneta_family). Marcos and Araneta were married in [Fiesole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiesole), Italy, on April 17, 1993. They have three sons: [Ferdinand Alexander III "Sandro"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandro_Marcos) (born 1994), Joseph Simon (born 1995) and [William Vincent "Vinny"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Vincent_Marcos) (born 1997).[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356] Although he is [Ilocano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilocano_people) by [ethnic ancestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_the_Philippines), he was brought up in a [Manileño household](72340.md) and does not speak the [Ilocano language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilocano_language).[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] The Marcos family maintains a residence in [Forbes Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_Park,_Makati), Makati.[^cite_note-359]

Aside from his common nickname "Bongbong", Marcos is known by his peers as "Bonggets".[^cite_note-Geronimo20151012-40] Marcos is an avid listener of [rock and roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_and_roll), [rhythm and blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhythm_and_blues), and [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) music. He once held a [record collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Record_collecting) in Malacañang Palace that he described as "the best record collection in the Philippines" but left it when his family was exiled from the country in 1986. He is a fan of [the Beatles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beatles), citing *[Sgt. Pepper's Lonely Hearts Club Band](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sgt._Pepper's_Lonely_Hearts_Club_Band)* as his favorite album of theirs, and often collects [the band's memorabilia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_the_Beatles). Marcos can also play the saxophone.[^cite_note-The_other_side_of_Bongbong-360]

Marcos exercises regularly and claims to abstain from consuming confections and soft drinks,[^cite_note-Geronimo20151012-40] and also enjoys cooking.[^cite_note-How_Marcos_learned_about_home-cooked_dishes-361] Marcos is also an avid reader, a [cinephile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinephile), and a gun enthusiast, where he holds a competition under his name.[^cite_note-Geronimo20151012-40][^cite_note-25_things-46][^cite_note-Filipino_politicians,_guns,_and_power-362] Marcos also follows [Formula One](10854.md) racing as a supporter of [Scuderia Ferrari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuderia_Ferrari); during his presidency, he attended the [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Singapore_Grand_Prix) and [2023 Singapore Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Singapore_Grand_Prix) with Prime Minister [Lee Hsien Loong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Hsien_Loong) and other foreign dignitaries.[^cite_note-363][^cite_note-Marcos_back_in_PH_on_Sunday,_says_Palace-364]

On March 31, 2020, Marcos's spokesperson confirmed that Marcos had tested positive for [COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_in_the_Philippines).[^cite_note-tests-positive-covid-19-365] Prior to getting tested, Marcos was reportedly experiencing chest pains after coming home from a trip to Spain. He has since recovered from the disease after testing negative on a [RT-PCR test](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_testing#Reverse_transcription_polymerase_chain_reaction_\(RT-PCR\)_test) on May 5, 2020, a month after testing positive for COVID-19.[^cite_note-recovery-covid-19-366] On July 8, 2022, Marcos's press secretary confirmed that Marcos had tested positive again for COVID-19 after experiencing slight fever.[^cite_note-367]

In January 2026, Marcos was diagnosed with [diverticulitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diverticulitis).[^cite_note-368]

### Alleged cocaine usage

On November 18, 2021, President [Rodrigo Duterte](7407293.md) claimed in a televised speech that a certain candidate for the [2022 Philippine presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Philippine_presidential_election) is allegedly using [cocaine](7701.md), hinting at the candidate using male pronouns on several instances. Furthermore, Duterte alleged that the candidate eluded law enforcement authorities by doing drugs on a private yacht and a plane.[^cite_note-369] Although he did not name the candidate, it was alluded that Duterte was referring to Marcos after he continued on his speech that the male candidate is a "weak leader" and has been "capitalizing on [his father](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_Marcos)'s accomplishments".[^cite_note-370] Prior to that, Duterte previously named Marcos a "weak leader who had done nothing" and a "spoiled child for being an only son".[^cite_note-371]

Days after Duterte's allegation, Marcos took a cocaine [drug test](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_test) through a urine sample at [St. Luke's Medical Center – Global City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Luke's_Medical_Center_%E2%80%93_Global_City) and submitted the negative result to law enforcement authorities with a follow-up online memo by the medical institution confirming the legitimacy of the test.[^cite_note-372]

Marcos responded that he did not feel that he was the one alluded to by President Duterte. According to [health care provider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care_provider) [American Addiction Centers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Addiction_Centers), after the last use, cocaine or its metabolites can show up on a blood or saliva test for up to two days, a urine test for up to three days, and a hair test for months to years.[^cite_note-373] In an interview with [CNN Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNN_Philippines) in April 2022, Marcos responded to Duterte's remarks on him being a "spoiled" and "weak leader", saying that the president was "playing politics" and was "always making sure everybody's thinking hard about what they're doing".[^cite_note-374]

In an interview with [ANC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABS-CBN_News_Channel) in May 2022, former senator [Nikki Coseteng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikki_Coseteng), who claimed to personally know Marcos, alleged that Marcos was a "lazy individual" who frequented [discos](19321330.md) and got high on illegal substances along with his socialite friends during his youth.[^cite_note-375] Marcos has neither denied nor confirmed Coseteng's allegations.[^cite_note-376]

In late January 2024, Marcos's alleged cocaine use was brought anew by Duterte, during a prayer rally against [Charter change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_reform_in_the_Philippines) in [Davao City](489401.md).[^cite_note-fentanyl-377] Duterte alleged that Marcos had once been included in the [Philippine Drug Enforcement Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Drug_Enforcement_Agency)'s (PDEA) drug watchlist (which the agency denied) and had been spotted using cocaine with his cohorts at a giant banana firm's plantation in [Davao del Norte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davao_del_Norte) when Duterte was Mayor. Duterte said that these companions of Marcos were already working for his administration, and cited this as the reason why he did not vote for him in the 2022 general election. When asked by Marcos to prove the allegations, Duterte retorted that it is incumbent upon him to prove the allegations by taking a drug test, since he is the one holding public office.[^cite_note-fentanyl-377] Marcos maintained he had never used illegal narcotics, let alone cocaine, and blamed Duterte's use of [fentanyl](141915.md) as a response. In Duterte's defense, he had used fentanyl because it was prescribed to him by a "Dr. Javier", his alleged physician at [St. Luke's Medical Center – Quezon City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Luke's_Medical_Center_%E2%80%93_Quezon_City), to alleviate pain from injuries sustained in a motorbike accident a few years ago.[^cite_note-fentanyl-377]

In April 2024, leaked documents from the [Philippine Drug Enforcement Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Drug_Enforcement_Agency) (PDEA) circulated online, linking Marcos and actress [Maricel Soriano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maricel_Soriano) to illegal drugs. The [Senate Committee on Public Order and Dangerous Drugs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Senate_Committee_on_Public_Order_and_Dangerous_Drugs), headed by Senator [Ronald dela Rosa](50966529.md), later conducted a hearing on the matter and invited vlogger Maharlika to explain her involvement in the online circulation of the documents. Former PDEA investigation agent Jonathan Morales declared that the documents were authentic; PDEA Director General Moro Virgilio Lazo, on the other hand, claimed the documents were fake.[^cite_note-378]

On May 20, 2024, the [Philippine Senate Committee on Public Order and Dangerous Drugs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Senate_Committee_on_Public_Order_and_Dangerous_Drugs) panel cited [PDEA agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Drug_Enforcement_Agency) Jonathan Morales in [contempt](7201.md) for '["continuously lying](23688.md)". Upon [Jinggoy Estrada](2395334.md)'s [motion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motion_\(parliamentary_procedure\)) and [seconded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_\(parliamentary_procedure\)) by [Ronald dela Rosa](50966529.md), Morales was ordered [detained](2896261.md) for [flipped-flopped](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flip-flop_\(politics\)) replies on [PDS](1845896.md), inter alia. Earlier, a former [National Police Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Police_Commission_\(Philippines\)) officer, Eric "Pikoy" Santiago was also held in contempt of the Senate for being a "liar".[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380] On May 23, 2024, Morales and Santiago were released from custody according to [Senate Sergeant-at-Arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serjeant-at-arms) Roberto Ancan.[^cite_note-381] In August 2024, [San Fernando, Pampanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Fernando,_Pampanga) Municipal Trial Court Branch 9 Acting Presiding Judge Jason Alquiroz convicted Morales of [perjury](23688.md) and sentenced him to four months [imprisonment](213806.md) and fined ₱1,000.[^cite_note-382]

In November 2025, Marcos' sister [Imee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imee_Marcos) alleged that their family knew about Marcos' use of illegal drugs, stating during a three-day [Iglesia ni Cristo](288343.md) rally in [Manila](184334.md) that "when Bongbong and I were still kids, the whole family already knew about his problem. In truth, the testimonies of my father regarding his attitude and actions can be read by everyone. Before, he was not my responsibility because we still had a father. Growing up, \[he] became more concerning."[^cite_note-383]

| [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Philippine_presidential_election) | [Vice President of the Philippines](2271639.md) |  | [IND](2172378.md)[^cite_note-387] | 14,155,344 | 34.47% | 2nd | —N/a | Lost |
| [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Philippine_presidential_election) | [President of the Philippines](271126.md) |  | [PFP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partido_Federal_ng_Pilipinas) | 31,629,783 | 58.77% | 1st | —N/a | Won |

Presiden Filipina Ferdinand Marcos tgl 23 Maret melantik puteranya yang berusia 24 tahun Ferdinand R Marcos Jr sebagai Gubernur provinsi Ilocos Norie \[sic] di Filipina bagian utara. Marcos muda itu menggantikan bibinya Ny. Elizabeth M. Rocka yang karena kesehatannya mengundurkan diri sebagai Gubernur. Marcos muda terpilih sebagai wakil gubernur dalam pemilihan umum 1980. Dalam pemilu itu pula bibinya memenangkan jabatan gubernur. \[Philippine President Ferdinand Marcos on March 23 inaugurated his 24-year-old son, Ferdinand R. Marcos Jr., as governor of the northern Philippine province of Ilocos Norie \[sic]. The younger Marcos succeeded his aunt, Mrs. Elizabeth M. Rocka, who resigned as governor due to health reasons.
